[Errno 2] No such file or directory`.

Owner file / owner surface:
Coordinator dispatch prompt / subagent cwd instruction. No source file owner.

5 Whys:

1. Why did the command fail? It ran from the parent repo folder where the
   runner path was not present.
2. Why did it run there? The subagent inherited a shell cwd that was not the
   assigned checkout.
3. Why did the prompt not prevent this? It named the checkout but did not force
   `Set-Location` as an executable first step.
4. Why is this an Andon? A coordinator error could be mistaken for Khaybar
   smoke evidence or a spent model attempt.
5. Why was recovery allowed? No run directory, output, records, model request,
   package, release, or provenance artifact existed, so no case smoke had been
   consumed.

Hansei:
Matrix subagent prompts must make cwd an executable precondition, not an
ambient assumption.

Countermeasure:
Closed the failed runner, verified the assigned run directory was absent, then
relaunched Khaybar with explicit `Set-Location` to the active checkout before
the command.

Smoke B command/result:

real case result under
`.daee/release-regression-smokes/20260605-061225-matrix-khaybar/`.

### Andon GATE62F-TRINITARIAN-M9-UNDECLARED-OPERATION

Symptom:
Trinitarian produced a 162336-byte compiled `output.md` and reached Stage 08
verifier handoff, but failed NLA semantic faithfulness because ACT rows used
`M9.category-or-referent-separation`.

Failed check:

```powershell
python tools\check_nla_decode_semantic_faithfulness.py --outputs .daee\release-regression-smokes\20260605-061225-matrix-trinitarian-j173\output.md
```

Failure:
ACT `²B₂` and `⁴B₁` operation `category-or-referent-separation` is not
declared by the formal owner contract; expected one of
`predication-repair`, `repair`, `sense-split`.

Owner file / owner surface:
Producer/scaffold and Stage 07 ACT projection surfaces:
`tools/run_staged_current_skill_smoke.py`,
`tools/build_staged_governed_output.py`, and M9 owner-operation vocabulary /
formal contract surfaces. The checker
`tools/check_nla_decode_semantic_faithfulness.py` remains strict.

5 Whys:

1. Why did Stage 08 fail? Public ACT rows used an M9 operation not declared by
   the formal owner contract.
2. Why did that operation appear? The producer/scaffold allowed the semantic
   result phrase `category-or-referent-separation` to occupy the operation
   slot.
3. Why is that wrong? The operation slot must be a callable owner operation;
   category/referent semantics belong in `π=`, `delta_result`, or public prose
   unless the owner contract explicitly licenses that operation.
4. Why did Gate62F not catch it before the model smoke? Gate62F added
   operation floors to the Stage 04 harness, but the NLA/formal contract path
   still has an M9 contract/list mismatch for this specific emitted operation.
5. Why is the root cause systemic? It is the DSL layer-separation failure:
   owner operation, semantic pressure, and result label can still drift apart
   after Stage 04 unless all projection/checker surfaces share the same
   owner-operation vocabulary.

Hansei:
The no-model repair correctly stopped several owner-operation drifts, but the
formal NLA contract remained an independent owner list. Gate62F reduced one
gap and exposed the next producer/checker asymmetry.

Countermeasure:
Open a no-model repair lane that unifies or cross-checks M9 operation floors
across Stage 04 guidance, Stage 07 projection, and NLA contract expectations.
Do not accept `category-or-referent-separation` in the operation slot unless an
owner decision expands the formal M9 operation contract with register-axis and
delta canaries. Prefer current contract operation `repair` or
`predication-repair` while retaining `category-separated` /
`referent-separated` in `delta_result`.

Smoke B command/result:
No countermeasure applied yet. Gemba replay command above reproduces FAIL.
Required Smoke B for closure: staged runner self-test plus NLA no-model canary
showing M9 undeclared operation rejected and M9 declared operation with
category/referent delta accepted.

Remaining blocker or closure:
Open. Blocks Trinitarian Stage 08, retained promotion, release matrix closure,
package, tag, GitHub Release, release assets, and provenance.

### Andon GATE62F-TRINITARIAN-M9-UNDECLARED-OPERATION - Closed

Symptom:
The Trinitarian Gate62F matrix output produced Stage 07 `output.md`, then
failed NLA because visible ACT rows used
`M9.category-or-referent-separation` as an operation.

Failed check:

```powershell
python tools\check_nla_decode_semantic_faithfulness.py --outputs .daee\release-regression-smokes\20260605-061225-matrix-trinitarian-j173\output.md
```

Owner file / owner surface:
`atomics/skill/references/diagnostics/delta-result-vocabulary.json`,
`tools/run_staged_current_skill_smoke.py`, and the M9 formal owner contract.

5 Whys:

1. Why did NLA fail? The ACT operation token was not declared by M9's formal
   owner contract.
2. Why was it emitted? The runtime guidance/vocabulary had allowed
   `category-or-referent-separation` in operation space.
3. Why is that wrong? `category-separated` and `referent-separated` are
   `delta_result` values; the callable M9 operation is `predication-repair`,
   `sense-split`, or `repair`.
4. Why did the canaries miss it? The controlled delta self-test checked M9
   deltas but did not reject a result label used as an operation.
5. Why is this DSL-relevant? The transition
   `owner operation -> ACT row -> controlled delta_result` was collapsed into
   one label, weakening typed reconstruction.

Hansei:
The model can sound formal while moving a state-change label into the operation
slot. The checker/scaffold must keep `owner.operation` and `delta_result`
separate.

Countermeasure:
Removed `category-or-referent-separation` from the M9 operation vocabulary and
added a no-model Stage 04 canary rejecting
`M9.category-or-referent-separation` while preserving M9 deltas such as
`category-separated` and `referent-separated`.

remain typed. The verifier cannot reconstruct closure from state strings that
are actually delta labels.

heads. Added a valid fixture where public detail follows `landed / ...`, and an
invalid fixture where `terminal_landed_*` is used as the state.

### Andon GATE62H-TRINITARIAN-PROOF-STACK-HIDDEN-SUPPORT-DELTA - Closed

Symptom:
Trinitarian produced Stage 07 `output.md`, then failed owner activation
ordering. The ACT row used `hidden-support-blocked` for proof-stack/backread
hidden-support pressure.


`tests/owner-activation-ordering/valid/proof-stack-backread-hidden-support-delta.md`,
and
`tests/owner-activation-ordering/invalid/proof-stack-backread-hidden-support-generic-delta.md`.

5 Whys:

1. Why did the check fail? The checker required the generic
   `hidden-support-blocked` token for a proof-stack/backread hidden-support
   pressure.
2. Why was that wrong? Proof-stack/backread hidden support is a proof-text
   hidden-support subclass, not generic source-order recoil.
3. Why did the system allow the mismatch? The pressure classifier only keyed on
   the narrower `proof-text-hidden-support` phrase.
4. Why was it not caught earlier? No no-model canary covered proof-stack or
   backread wording for the same DSL transition.
5. What countermeasure prevents recurrence? Canonicalize the pressure text and
   require `proof-text-hidden-support-blocked` whenever hidden-support pressure
   is proof-text/proof-stack/backread-shaped.

Hansei:
The checker was strict in the right place but too literal about the pressure
class. That incentivized model output to choose a weaker generic delta token for
a more specific proof-stack transition.

Countermeasure:
`source_recoil_delta_errors()` now distinguishes proof-text/proof-stack/backread
hidden support from generic source-order recoil, and valid/invalid canaries lock
the delta-result split.

### Andon GATE62H-PUSH-TRANSPORT-AUTH-BLOCKER - Open

Symptom:
The Gate62H local commit was created/amended after Smoke B and safety scans,
but push did not complete.

Failed check / push attempt:

```powershell
gh auth status
ssh -T -o BatchMode=yes -o StrictHostKeyChecking=accept-new git@github.com
git push git@github.com:theislampill/daee-epistemics.git HEAD:main
mcp GitHub _update_ref main -> local Gate62H commit
GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0 GCM_INTERACTIVE=Never git -c credential.helper= -c core.askPass= ls-remote origin HEAD
```

Observed results:

- `gh auth status`: keyring token invalid for `theislampill`.
- `ssh`: sandbox shim at `C:\Users\theis\.sbx-denybin\ssh.bat`; SSH push failed
  with `fatal: Could not read from remote repository`.
- GitHub connector `_update_ref`: HTTP 422 / `Object does not exist`, because
  the local commit object has not been uploaded to GitHub.
- Native HTTPS push was not retried after the Windows `git-remote-https.exe`
  memory-access popup; the non-native API/connector path was attempted first.
- After the amended Gate62H commit, a non-interactive/no-credential-helper
  HTTPS probe avoided the popup but could not reach GitHub:
  `Failed to connect to github.com port 443 via 127.0.0.1`.

Owner file / owner surface:
Host Git/GitHub transport/auth boundary, not a repo source/checker surface.

5 Whys:

1. Why did push fail? The remote branch cannot be moved to a commit object that
   GitHub does not have.
2. Why was the commit object missing remotely? The native HTTPS push path is
   unsafe/unavailable on this host, and the GitHub API path could not upload the
   local commit through `gh` because its keyring token is invalid.
3. Why did SSH not solve it? The sandbox exposes `ssh` as a deny shim and the
   repository could not be accessed over SSH.
4. Why could the connector not finish the push directly? `_update_ref` can move
   refs but cannot make an absent local commit object exist without creating the
   blob/tree/commit objects first.
5. What countermeasure prevents recurrence? Reauthenticate `gh`, provide a
   usable non-keyring GitHub token/API push path, or restore direct GitHub
   network transport from the sandbox; then push the existing local commit or
   recreate its tree via Git Data API.

Hansei:
The code lane was clean, but transport resilience is still brittle on this
Windows host. Native HTTPS push can surface an OS popup; SSH is sandbox-denied;
the connector can authenticate but did not have the local commit object.

Countermeasure already applied or proposed:
Applied: avoided another credential-helper HTTPS push after the popup, tried
connector and SSH alternatives, used a non-interactive/no-helper HTTPS probe,
and preserved the local commit. Proposed: owner refreshes GitHub auth
(`gh auth login` / valid token), authorizes a usable API token, or restores
direct GitHub network transport so the existing commit can be pushed without
the failing helper path.

Smoke B command/result:
All Gate62H no-model Smoke B and safety scans passed before commit; see
Gate62H Full Smoke B And Safety above.

Remaining blocker or closure:
Open transport/auth blocker. Do not enter a new model-smoke matrix, Stage 8,
retained promotion, package, tag, release, assets, or provenance until the
Gate62H commit is pushed and CI/Pages are green.

   lexicographically, so `sort` appears before `source-order`.

   newly admitted SOURCE operations.
5. What prevents recurrence? Self-test expectations must mirror deterministic
   generated order when asserting vocabulary guidance, and the canary now
   checks the actual sorted SOURCE operation surface.

Hansei:
The checker/canary was correct in principle but imprecise about the generator's
stable ordering. The failure was useful because it proved the guidance surface
was being checked, but the assertion was too hand-authored.

Countermeasure:

```text
SOURCE operations: authority-order-repair, sort, source-order, source-order-repair, status
```

Owner file / owner surface:
Host Codex CLI execution boundary and matrix coordinator invocation. The repo

5 Whys:

Hansei:
The matrix coordinator reached for parallel case execution before verifying the
model subprocess OS boundary after the Codex app restart. The no-model harness
was green, but that did not prove the live model transport path.

Countermeasure proposed:
Before spending the next matrix, run a tiny transport preflight from the same
execution boundary as the matrix runner:


### Andon GATE62H-CI-SELFTEST-CODEX-PATH-COUPLING - Closed

Symptom:
Commit `9e14b00eb03c9592f25a948bfb14fd192934829b` pushed successfully and
Pages passed, but GitHub Actions CI run `27026755615` failed in
`runtime-checks`, step `Build and verify runtime`.

Failed check:

```text
python tools/run_staged_current_skill_smoke.py --self-test
```

CI log:

```text
staged current-skill harness: BLOCKED: codex CLI not found on PATH; model smoke is blocked by harness/credential environment
```

Owner file / owner surface:
`tools/run_staged_current_skill_smoke.py` no-model self-test. The live runner
must still require Codex CLI for real model smokes, but CI self-test must remain
no-model and must not require Codex to be installed.

5 Whys:

1. Why did CI fail? The no-model staged current-skill self-test exited with a
   Codex CLI missing HarnessError.
2. Why did the self-test look for Codex? The new boundary assertion called
   `build_codex_command`, and that helper resolved `shutil.which("codex")`.
3. Why was that wrong for CI? CI runtime-checks intentionally run no-model
   harness validation without Codex CLI on `PATH`.
4. Why was this not caught locally? Local Smoke B ran in a Codex Desktop
   environment where `codex` exists, so the self-test portability bug was
   masked.
5. What countermeasure prevents recurrence? Let the no-model self-test inject a
   placeholder Codex executable into the command builder, while keeping the live
   `invoke_codex` path responsible for resolving a real Codex CLI.

Hansei:
The first countermeasure protected the live subprocess boundary but accidentally
pulled live-host availability into a no-model CI self-test. No-model canaries
must assert command shape without depending on the external model runner.

Countermeasure applied:
`build_codex_command` now accepts an optional `codex_executable` override for
no-model command-shape assertions. The self-test passes `codex_executable="codex"`
to inspect the generated command without requiring Codex CLI on `PATH`. The live
`invoke_codex` path remains unchanged in behavior: real model smokes still
resolve a real Codex executable and fail honestly if the CLI is unavailable.

### Andon GATE62H-MATRIX-SECULARISM-STAGE02-BURDEN-FLOOR-ID-POLLUTION - Closed No-Model

Symptom:
The Secularism smoke exited with `staged current-skill smoke: FAIL` before an
accepted Stage 02 record and before `output.md`. The Stage 02 model response had
`status: pass`, but `burden_floor[0]` was a prose-decorated register/join-key
hybrid:

```text
¹B / ξ warrant-authority burden: secularism/public reason installs a source-authority tribunal while presenting as neutral
```

Failed check:

```text
C:\Users\theis\.cache\codex-runtimes\codex-primary-runtime\dependencies\python\python.exe tools\run_staged_current_skill_smoke.py --case-name matrix-secularism-gate62h ...
```

First failure:

```text
stage-02 burden_floor[0] string cannot be normalized to one canonical burden id
```

Owner file / owner surface:
Stage 02 diagnostic-IR producer/scaffold and the staged runner Stage 02
normalizer in `tools/run_staged_current_skill_smoke.py`. Canonical source
owners are the generated runtime Stage 02 prompt/source surfaces under
`atomics/skill/` after owner trace.

5 Whys:
1. Why did Secularism fail? Stage 02 emitted a burden-floor entry that could
   not normalize to a single canonical burden ID.
2. Why was it not canonical? The model fused the burden ID, register axis, and
   burden prose into the `burden_floor` join-key field.
3. Why did the model fuse them? Recent formalism guidance emphasized live
   registers, but the Stage 02 output contract did not force `burden_floor` to
   stay as bare IDs with labels/registers in separate detail fields.
4. Why did no canary catch this before a live smoke? Existing body_ref
   pollution canaries focused on Stage 04 ACT/body_ref and public joins, not
   Stage 02 `burden_floor` dialect pollution.
5. What countermeasure prevents recurrence? Add a no-model Stage 02 canary that
   rejects register/prose-decorated `burden_floor` values and accepts bare
   `B1`, `B2`, etc. with register/prose in `burden_floor_details`.

Hansei:
Formal register awareness can backfire if the stage contract does not keep
identity fields, register axes, and prose details in separate slots. The repair
must preserve burden richness while keeping the join key bare.

Countermeasure proposed:
Open a no-model Stage 02 DSL/IR canary/checker repair. Do not loosen the
normalizer to accept polluted IDs as proof. Teach the producer/scaffold to emit
bare burden IDs and put register/prose labels in detail fields.

Countermeasure applied:
`tools/run_staged_current_skill_smoke.py` now tells Stage 02 to emit
`burden_floor` as bare canonical IDs only, such as `B1` and `B2`, and to place
public labels, register axes, source labels, and prose in `burden_floor_details`.
The Stage 02 normalizer now rejects non-bare burden-floor strings rather than
normalizing them by substring. `tools/check_staged_runtime_handshake.py` now
checks the same invariant on records and verifies that any
`burden_floor_details[].burden_id` is canonical and points back to the bare
`burden_floor`.

No-model canaries:
`tests/staged-runtime-handshake/valid/stage02-bare-burden-floor-details.json`
proves bare IDs can carry rich register/prose detail rows without polluting the
join key. `tests/staged-runtime-handshake/invalid/stage02-burden-floor-public-label-pollution.json`
rejects a public/register/prose burden-floor label without using a case name.
The staged harness self-test also rejects both `B1: ...` prose labels and
`¹B / ξ ...` public/register labels in `burden_floor`.

Repair-internal Andon:
The first focused post-patch `check_staged_runtime_handshake.py` run failed
because the new valid fixture used `mode: no-model-fixture` with a stage-local
`stage_scope`. 5 Whys: the fixture copied a prefix-record shape but kept
no-model metadata; the checker treats `stage_scope` as valid only for
`staged-current-skill-stage-local-smoke`; this would have made the canary test
fixture dialect rather than the Stage 02 invariant; Smoke A did not include the
new fixture because it did not exist yet; the countermeasure was to switch both
new neutral fixtures to the existing stage-local model-mode dialect, add
`model_scope`, and use model-mode non-claims. The rerun passed.

Smoke B command/result:
PASS:


state remains `held-with-reason`, `carried-PARTIAL`, `carried-RECURSE`, or
otherwise HOLD/PARTIAL. It also instructs the model that complete closure must

formal operation token. That created a token-projection-without-transition risk:
the public body could be good while the typed owner operation remained
ambiguous.

as stale-artifact evidence and require future outputs to use bare `body_ref`
plus separate SOURCE owner alias, controlled formal operation, controlled
delta_result, and dereferenced body evidence.

## Gate62J Trinitarian Stage07 SOURCE/Layer-B Operation-Mass Repair - Closed Pushed/Green

Status:
No-model checker/canary repair closed locally with full Smoke B and safety
scans passing, then pushed as `7c81bc2`. GitHub CI run `27039360853` and Pages
run `27039360392` both completed successfully for that commit. No model smoke,
Stage 8, retained promotion, package, tag, release assets, public sample
publication, or provenance is opened by this repair.

Classification:
`checker/normalizer failed to preserve the DSL`: the preserved Trinitarian
output contained a dereferenced SOURCE/authority-order public body with direct
source first, supporting sources below until argued, and proposed synthesis
below the source under examination. The manual render checker still failed it
because the SOURCE negation guard treated legitimate boundary prose, "the text
does not perform the later synthesis," as if the owner operation itself did not
perform authority-order repair.

DSL transition protected:

```text
body_ref
-> owner_id=authority-order-repair / SOURCE
-> operation=authority-order-repair
-> delta_result=authority-order-repaired
-> dereferenced public Layer B body performs governing-source authority ordering
-> Land(Bn) licensing
```

Smoke A / Gemba:

```powershell
python tools\check_manual_smoke_render_contract.py --outputs .daee\release-regression-smokes\20260605-150507-matrix-trinitarian-j173\output.md
python tools\check_mrp_generated_burden.py --outputs .daee\release-regression-smokes\20260605-150507-matrix-trinitarian-j173\output.md --show-advisories
```

Result:

```text
manual smoke render contract: FAIL
- Layer B submove mass insufficient: required fields are present, but owner/TTP work is conclusion-shaped rather than operation-shaped
- source-status/authority-order submove did not sort authority or prevent hidden support.
- Land(²B) claimed without mass-sufficient Layer B treatment
- Baseline burden treated as if low-mass without diagnostic license
- ACT ²B₂ SOURCE authority-order-repair lacks authority/rank/tribunal/source-sovereignty transition evidence

MRP generated-burden check: PASS
Hosted/live outputs checked: 1
```

5 Whys:
1. Why did Stage07 fail? The manual render checker rejected the public
   SOURCE/authority-order body for ²B.
2. Why did it reject the body? The SOURCE formal-transition predicate saw a
   negation hit in the operation body and suppressed the authority-order proof.
3. Why was the negation hit wrong? The prose said the source text does not
   perform a later synthesis by itself; that is a valid boundary claim, not a
   claim that the owner operation failed to perform authority ordering.
4. Why did canaries miss it? Existing SOURCE canaries protected compact
   operation/delta pairing and body-backed evidence, but did not cover
   governing-source/proposed-synthesis authority ordering with a legitimate
   "does not perform later synthesis" boundary sentence.
5. What prevents recurrence? Narrow the SOURCE negation guard to reject
   statements that the operation/body does not perform SOURCE/authority repair,
   while adding a valid governing-authority canary and an invalid negated
   operation-body canary.

Hansei:
The checker was strict for the right reason but too blunt at the sentence
level. It could incentivize rewriting public prose away from careful source
boundaries just to avoid the word "not." The countermeasure keeps SOURCE proof
strict while distinguishing source-boundary negation from owner-operation
negation.

Countermeasure applied:
- Narrowed `SOURCE_REPAIR_NEGATED_EVIDENCE_RE` in
  `tools/check_manual_smoke_render_contract.py` so "does not perform" only
  rejects when it negates SOURCE/authority repair work rather than any sentence
  about what a cited source text does not do.
- Added neutral valid fixture
  `tests/manual-smoke-render/valid/source-authority-governing-order-boundary.md`
  for direct-source-first / support-text-lower / proposed-synthesis-lower
  authority ordering.
- Added neutral invalid fixture
  `tests/manual-smoke-render/invalid/source-authority-negated-operation-body.md`
  to preserve the false-pass guard when a body explicitly says it does not
  perform authority ordering work.

Initial local replay:

```powershell

python tools\check_manual_smoke_render_contract.py --outputs .daee\release-regression-smokes\20260605-150507-matrix-trinitarian-j173\output.md
python -m py_compile tools\check_manual_smoke_render_contract.py
```


## Gate62I Stage03->04 Owner-Operation/Delta Availability Repair - Closed Pushed/Green

Status:
No-model source/checker/fixture repair closed locally with full Smoke B and
safety scans passing, then pushed as `056d302`. GitHub CI run `27038336998`
and Pages run `27038336511` both completed successfully for that commit. No
model smoke, Stage 8, retained promotion, package, tag, release assets, public
sample publication, or provenance is opened by this repair.

Classification:
`producer/scaffold failed to emit the DSL` plus
`token-projection without transition evidence`: Stage 03 could route owner
bodies into Stage 04 without proving that the route had a callable owner
operation and controlled owner-local `delta_result`, so Stage 04 correctly
refused fake `Land(...)` for the latest TST and Secularism matrix artifacts.

DSL transition protected:

```text
burden_id
-> selected route / owner family
-> callable owner operation
-> ACT row with bare body_ref
-> controlled delta_result
-> MRP / NAR / field_witness / public-output witness eligibility
```

Countermeasure applied:
- Added controlled owner aliases, operations, and owner-local deltas for the
  V2 reconstituting-reason and pattern-profiling owner families in the canonical
  delta-result vocabulary.
- Hardened `tools/check_staged_runtime_handshake.py` so executable Stage 03
  routes must have owner-operation/delta vocabulary coverage unless explicitly
  held, partial, inert, or not-loaded.
- Hardened Stage 04 ACT validation so row objects and public ACT details are
  checked against owner-operation and owner-local delta vocabulary.
- Updated the staged current-skill self-test scaffold so unmapped routed owners
  are instructed to emit HOLD/PARTIAL / controlled-vocabulary-gap rather than
  fake Land.
- Added neutral no-model canaries for valid V2/pattern owner execution,
  invalid executable unmapped owner Land, and valid HOLD/PARTIAL routing when
  an owner is not executable.
- Refreshed stale valid fixture dialects and the bound public-output SHA after
  replacing legacy SOURCE `source-order-landed` tokens with controlled current
  SOURCE deltas.

Result:
PASS. Staged runtime canaries checked 11 valid / 68 invalid fixtures, and the
preserved TST/Secularism handoff records replayed as valid custody evidence.
The staged current-skill self-test passed at
`.daee/validation/staged-current-skill-harness-self-test-ab94f16d72f14f96b56e374c0d9b597e`.
MRP generated-burden fixtures passed 21 valid / 47 invalid plus output gates;
manual render fixtures passed 6 valid / 29 invalid; compiled runtime freshness,
py_compile, TTP operator contracts, field-operator architecture, docs freshness,
docs interactions, docs Output Grapher smoke, `git diff --check`,
forbidden-path scan, high-signal secret scan, and ActiveGraph JSON parsing all
passed. The first docs freshness check failed earlier because generated public
docs had not yet been rebuilt after atomics changed; `python
tools\build_docs_index.py` regenerated `docs/index.html` and
`docs/daee-epistemics-pipeline.html`, then docs freshness/interactions/Grapher
passed. The first staged-runtime replay failed one bound public-output hash
after a fixture token correction; the SHA was updated to match the refreshed
fixture body and replay passed.

5 Whys:
1. Why did the matrix fail? Stage 04 could not legally land selected V2 and
   pattern-profiling routes.
2. Why were the routes illegal? The selected owner bodies lacked controlled
   callable operation/delta mappings at the Stage03->04 boundary.
3. Why did this surface only in live smokes? Existing canaries checked several
   owner families and stale dialects but did not require every executable
   routed owner to prove operation/delta availability or become HOLD/PARTIAL.
4. Why must the repair stay generic? Case-name fixes would violate the central
   DSL/IR invariant; the proof behavior must depend on route/owner/delta state,
   not TST/Secularism strings.
5. What prevents recurrence? The checker and self-test now require executable
   routed owners to have controlled operation/delta coverage, while neutral
   canaries cover the valid Land, valid HOLD/PARTIAL, and invalid fake-Land
   shapes.

Hansei:
The stricter Stage 04 failure was useful, but the scaffold still let Stage 03
select owners whose executable status was ambiguous. The repair standardizes
owner availability at the typed transition boundary instead of teaching the
model to write thinner public prose or adding broad aliases.

Anti-slimming check:
No model output was regenerated in this lane. The repair does not reduce
burden, generated-burden, ACT, MRP, NAR, field_witness, or public-witness
coverage; it blocks fake execution for unmapped owners and requires explicit
HOLD/PARTIAL when the typed owner transition is not available.

Remaining blocker or closure:

availability gap at `056d302`. The later Gate62J no-model repair closed the
preserved Trinitarian Stage07 SOURCE/Layer-B operation-mass Andon at

promotion, package, tag, release, release assets, public sample publication,
and provenance remain closed until an authorized post-repair model matrix or
release gate proves the next boundary.

Status:

does not rerun the four-smoke matrix, and does not open Stage 8, retained

already enforced the compact SOURCE formal-delta pairing, but

DSL transition protected:

```text
-> owner_id=SOURCE/source-status-repair/authority-order-repair

-> controlled delta_result=authority-order-repaired | source-order-repaired
```

even though `²B₁[source-status-repair.source-order]` used the compact

`⁵B₂[authority-order-repair.source-order]` artifact is invalid for
`authority-order-repaired`.


Failed check:
Before this patch, the manual render checker did not fail the
`source-order-repaired` / broad `source-order` baseline-public row. The old

Owner file / owner surface:

`tests/manual-smoke-render/valid/source-authority-formal-transition.md`,

`tests/manual-smoke-render/invalid/source-authority-compact-delta-broad-operation.md`,
and
`tests/manual-smoke-render/invalid/source-lineage-compact-delta-authority-only-body.md`.

5 Whys:
1. Why did the public-render gap exist? The manual checker validated public
   operation mass but did not parse ACT rows as SOURCE formal transition facts.
2. Why did that matter? Compact deltas such as `source-order-repaired` and
   `authority-order-repaired` are only projections of typed SOURCE transitions;
   token presence plus polished public prose is not proof.
3. Why was the MRP checker insufficient? MRP replay caught generated-burden
   stale dialects, but a baseline/public render row could still be evaluated by
   the manual checker without the same formal pair invariant.
4. Why must the fix stay narrow? Accepting broad `source-order` or `sort` as
   proof for compact formal deltas would collapse source-order and
   authority-order and weaken the DSL layer separation.
5. What countermeasure prevents recurrence? The manual checker now parses
   public ACT rows, rejects polluted or mismatched `body_ref`, applies
   SOURCE operation/delta vocabulary and formal pair checks, and dereferences
   the public Layer B body before accepting compact SOURCE repair deltas.

Hansei:
The previous SOURCE repair closed Stage 04/MRP surfaces but left a public-render
checker asymmetry. That could incentivize a model to produce cleaner public
prose while preserving stale operation/delta dialect. The repair moves the same
formal invariant into the public projection checker instead of adding runtime
case wording.

Countermeasure:
- Added `public_source_formal_transition_errors()` to
  `tools/check_manual_smoke_render_contract.py`.
- Added neutral valid SOURCE public-render canaries for
  `authority-order-repair / authority-order-repaired` and
  `source-order-repair / source-order-repaired`.
- Added neutral invalid canaries for a compact authority delta carried by broad
  `source-order`, and for a source-order compact delta whose body only performs
  authority/rank prose.
- Preserved bare `body_ref`; no owner/operation/transition notation is accepted
  inside the join key.
- Added no case-name or filename gates.

Andon GATE63-SHARED-STAGE05-TERMINAL-DETAIL-DIALECT:

Symptom:
TST, Khaybar, and Secularism produced `output.md`, passed Stage 01-08 records,
built verifier sidecars, and passed direct validators, but the smoke command
exited `1` because `check_staged_runtime_handshake.py` rejected Stage05
`terminal_state_details[*].terminal_state` dialect as missing `.state`.

Failed check:
`python tools\check_staged_runtime_handshake.py --records <case>\records\staged-handoff-record.json`.

Owner file / owner surface:
`tools/run_staged_current_skill_smoke.py`,
`tools/check_staged_runtime_handshake.py`, and staged-runtime-handshake
fixtures for Stage05 terminal-state details.

5 Whys:
1. Why did three rows fail after Stage08? The final handoff checker looked for
   `terminal_state_details[*].state`.
2. Why did the records not satisfy that field? Live Stage05 records emitted
   `terminal_state`, matching Stage06/NAR terminology, inside detail rows.
3. Why did producer/scaffold allow the dialect split? Stage05 validation
   normalizes top-level `terminal_states` but does not normalize or validate
   `terminal_state_details`.
4. Why did no fixture catch it? Existing no-model fixtures used `.state` and
   did not include a live-style `.terminal_state` detail-row dialect.
5. What countermeasure prevents recurrence? Add a generic no-model canary and
   strict producer/checker normalization so `terminal_state_details` use a
   controlled terminal state that matches top-level `terminal_states`, without
   accepting uncontrolled or conflicting state aliases.

Hansei:
The runner could assemble sidecars while its own final record contract remained
split across `state` and `terminal_state`. That makes good rows look failed and
risks later broadening by accepting either word without proving controlled
terminal-state identity.

Countermeasure:
Open one generic no-model Stage05 terminal-state detail schema repair before
any new model matrix. Do not rerun model smokes until the no-model canary,
Smoke B, safety scans, commit/push, and CI/Pages pass.

SOURCE owner-activation ordering checker/scaffold surfaces for hidden-support
or source-order recoil pressure.

5 Whys:
1. Why did Trinitarian fail? The output used the wrong SOURCE delta for a
   hidden-support/source-order recoil activation.
2. Why did that matter? `source-order-repaired` or adjacent SOURCE deltas do
   not prove hidden-support blocking when the pressure is hidden support.
3. Why did the no-model repair not prevent this row? Prior SOURCE repairs
   enforced formal transitions and public body evidence, but this row exposed a
   narrower pressure-to-delta assignment requirement inside owner activation
   ordering.
4. Why is this case-specific? The other three rows did not hit this checker

5. What prevents recurrence? Add a neutral no-model canary requiring
   hidden-support/source-order recoil pressure to carry `hidden-support-blocked`
   or an explicitly licensed hidden-support delta before release validation
   accepts the activation.

Hansei:
The repair should not loosen SOURCE deltas. It must preserve the typed
pressure-to-delta relation so compact source-order repairs do not launder
hidden-support work.

Countermeasure:
Defer patching this case-specific repair until after the shared Stage05 schema
repair, because the shared checker/producer asymmetry blocks multiple otherwise
passing rows.

Decision:
Multiple cases fail on the same shared owner surface, so prioritize one generic

## Gate63A Stage05 Terminal-State Detail Schema Repair - Closed Pushed Green

Status:
Closed as a no-model checker/producer contract repair. Commit `77de2d2`
(`fix: accept controlled stage05 terminal detail dialect`) was pushed to
`origin/main`; GitHub CI run `27041988204` and Pages run `27041987580` both
completed successfully on that head SHA.

Protected DSL transition:
`burden_id -> Stage05 terminal_states[Bn] -> terminal_state_details row ->
Stage06/NAR terminal_state mirror -> Stage07 release_terminal_states ->
Stage08 sidecar eligibility`.

Symptom:
Gate63 TST, Khaybar, and Secularism records carried controlled Stage05 terminal
states in `terminal_state_details[*].terminal_state`, but
`check_staged_runtime_handshake.py` accepted only the older fixture key
`state`, causing otherwise Stage08/direct-validator-passing rows to fail.

Failed check:
`python tools\check_staged_runtime_handshake.py --records .daee\release-regression-smokes\20260605-171351-matrix-<case>\records\staged-handoff-record.json`.

Owner file / owner surface:
`tools/run_staged_current_skill_smoke.py`,
`tools/check_staged_runtime_handshake.py`,
`tests/staged-runtime-handshake/valid/source-order-basic.json`, and
`tests/staged-runtime-handshake/invalid/stage05-terminal-state-detail-conflict.json`.

5 Whys:
1. Why did the rows fail? The checker required `.state` inside each detail row.
2. Why did live records use `.terminal_state`? The broader staged runtime and
   NAR surfaces use `terminal_state`, and Stage05 did not constrain optional
   detail-row spelling.
3. Why did producer normalization not fix it? `normalize_stage05_mrp_fields()`
   validated top-level `terminal_states` but ignored `terminal_state_details`.
4. Why did fixtures not catch it? Existing valid Stage05 fixtures used `.state`
   only, so they did not model the live dialect emitted by current smokes.
5. What prevents recurrence? The producer now normalizes live-style
   `.terminal_state` to `.state` only when controlled and matching top-level
   `terminal_states`, while the checker accepts either spelling only under the
   same equality condition and rejects conflicts.

Hansei:
This was not a model semantic failure in the three successful rows; it was a
schema/dialect split between the producer, checker, and fixtures. The repair
must not loosen terminal closure: terminal-state detail remains a controlled
state mirror, not a place for delta_result or prose outcome laundering.

Countermeasure:
- Updated Stage05 scaffold wording to require controlled `state` in
  `terminal_state_details`, with `terminal_state` normalized only as an
  identical live-style mirror.
- Added producer normalization/rejection for `terminal_state_details` rows.
- Updated the handshake checker to read `terminal_state` only when `.state` is
  absent, reject conflicts, and require the selected state to match top-level
  `terminal_states`.
- Converted a neutral valid fixture to the live-style `terminal_state` detail
  dialect.
- Added a neutral invalid fixture where `state` and `terminal_state` conflict.

Anti-slimming check:
No model output was regenerated or shortened. The repair changes only
checker/producer schema handling for ignored evidence records and no-model
fixtures; it does not alter burden selection, ACT rows, MRP/NAR mirrors,
field_witness, public output, or sidecar content.

## Gate63B SOURCE Hidden-Support Delta Repair - Closed Pushed Green

Status:
Closed as a no-model producer/checker contract repair. Commit `f294641`
(`fix: enforce source hidden-support delta`) was pushed to `origin/main`;
GitHub CI run `27042847689` and Pages run `27042841583` both completed
successfully on that head SHA.

Protected DSL transition:
`burden_id -> selected SOURCE owner family -> pressure classification ->
owner-local delta_result -> ACT row -> Stage06/NAR mirror -> field_witness
owner_activations -> Stage07 owner activation ordering`.

Symptom:
Gate63 Trinitarian produced `output.md` and passed direct manual/MRP/graph
validators, but Stage07 release validation failed
`check_owner_activation_ordering.py --require-plan` because
`owner_activations[5]` carried `pressure:
hidden-support-and-source-function-pressure` with `delta_result:
authority-order-separated`. The row was vocabulary-valid SOURCE output, but it
did not carry the hidden-support delta required by its own typed pressure.

Failed check:
`python tools\check_owner_activation_ordering.py --require-plan --outputs
.daee\release-regression-smokes\20260605-171351-matrix-trinitarian-j173\output.md`
-> FAIL: `owner_activations[5]: source-order recoil or hidden-support pressure
must use delta_result token 'hidden-support-blocked'`.

Owner file / owner surface:
`tools/delta_result_vocabulary.py`,
`tools/check_owner_activation_ordering.py`,
`tools/run_staged_current_skill_smoke.py`, and
`tests/owner-activation-ordering/plan-required/{valid,invalid}` fixtures for
SOURCE hidden-support pressure-to-delta assignment.

Failure class:
Producer/scaffold failed to emit or preserve the typed SOURCE pressure-to-delta
relation before Stage07. The checker was already strict; the gap was that the
producer accepted a vocabulary-valid but pressure-invalid SOURCE delta through
Stage04/Stage06/NAR.

5 Whys:
1. Why did Trinitarian fail Stage07? A SOURCE-family hidden-support pressure
   used `authority-order-separated` instead of `hidden-support-blocked`.
2. Why was that invalid? Authority-order separation does not prove hidden
   support was blocked when the row's own `pressure` names hidden support.
3. Why did it reach Stage07? The staged producer checked owner-local SOURCE
   vocabulary and SOURCE formal repair pairs, but did not enforce the narrower
   pressure-to-delta predicate before mirroring ACT into NAR/field_witness.
4. Why did existing no-model fixtures not stop it earlier? The checker fixtures
   covered source-order recoil and proof-text hidden support, but there was no
   neutral plan-required fixture for the current authority-order owner alias
   with hidden-support pressure and a wrong authority-order delta.
5. What prevents recurrence? Move the SOURCE hidden-support/source-order-recoil
   pressure-to-delta predicate into the shared delta vocabulary helper, make the
   staged producer call it for Stage04 ACT rows and Stage06/NAR rows, and add
   neutral valid/invalid plan-required fixtures for the authority-hidden-support
   shape.

Hansei:
The repair must not loosen `check_owner_activation_ordering.py`, and it must not
canonicalize the wrong token into a passing token. A row that says its pressure
is hidden support must use the hidden-support delta or fail before it becomes
release proof. This preserves DSL layer separation: pressure is not operation,
operation is not delta_result, and a vocabulary-valid token is not proof unless
it matches the typed transition.

Countermeasure:
- Added `source_pressure_delta_errors()` to
  `tools/delta_result_vocabulary.py` as the shared predicate for SOURCE
  hidden-support/source-order-recoil pressure.
- Updated `tools/check_owner_activation_ordering.py` to reuse the shared
  predicate without weakening the checker.
- Updated `tools/run_staged_current_skill_smoke.py` so Stage04 ACT rows and
  Stage06/NAR rows reject SOURCE hidden-support pressure unless the
  delta_result is `hidden-support-blocked`, or
  `proof-text-hidden-support-blocked` for proof-text/proof-stack/backread
  hidden-support.
- Added Stage04 and Stage06 runner self-test canaries for the current neutral
  authority-hidden-support wrong-delta shape.
- Added neutral plan-required owner-ordering fixtures:
  `authority-hidden-support-delta.md` (valid) and
  `authority-hidden-support-wrong-delta.md` (invalid).

Anti-slimming check:
No model output was regenerated or shortened. The preserved Trinitarian output
still fails the same Stage07 owner-ordering gate. The repair only prevents the
same invalid typed transition from being accepted earlier by Stage04/Stage06/NAR
producer surfaces; it does not reduce burden, generated-burden, submove,
field_witness, or public-output witness coverage.

```powershell
python tools\run_staged_current_skill_smoke.py --self-test
python tools\check_owner_activation_ordering.py
python tools\check_staged_runtime_handshake.py
python tools\check_mrp_generated_burden.py
python tools\check_manual_smoke_render_contract.py
python tools\build_docs_index.py --check
python tools\check_docs_index_interactions.py
python tools\check_docs_output_grapher_smoke.py
python tools\check_compiled_runtime_freshness.py
python tools\check_owner_activation_ordering.py --require-plan --outputs .daee\release-regression-smokes\20260605-171351-matrix-trinitarian-j173\output.md
```

Result:
PASS for py_compile, staged self-test, owner-activation fixture suite

Remaining blocker or closure:

### Andon GATE64-SHARED-STAGE05-BASIS-DIALECT

Status:
Open; blocks another model matrix until closed by a no-model repair, Smoke B,
push, and green CI/Pages.

Symptom:
Trinitarian and TST both passed Stage01-04, produced Stage05 responses, then
failed before Stage06 because `terminal_state_details[*].basis` was a
non-empty array while the runner/checker accepted only a non-empty string.
Gemba confirmed Trinitarian Stage05 detail rows carried array basis counts
`2,2,2,2`, and TST carried array basis counts `2,2,1,2`.

Failed check:
Stage05 harness validation inside `tools\run_staged_current_skill_smoke.py`:
`stage-05 terminal_state_details[0].basis must be non-empty when present`.

Owner file / owner surface:
`tools/run_staged_current_skill_smoke.py`,
`tools/check_staged_runtime_handshake.py`, and neutral
`tests/staged-runtime-handshake/` fixtures for Stage05
`terminal_state_details[*].basis`.

DSL transition protected:
`burden_id -> Stage05 terminal_states[Bn] -> terminal_state_details row ->
basis/evidence for terminal state -> Stage06/NAR mirror -> Stage07 release
terminal states`.

Failure class:
checker/normalizer failed to preserve the DSL shape emitted by the staged
producer. This is schema evidence, not a model semantic failure inside a valid
trace.

5 Whys:
1. Why did the two cases fail? The Stage05 validator required `basis` to be a
   non-empty string.
2. Why did valid-looking Stage05 responses fail? The model emitted non-empty
   arrays of basis clauses, preserving evidence but using a structured list
   dialect.
3. Why was that dialect rejected? Gate63A repaired `state` versus
   `terminal_state`, but did not audit or fixture the adjacent optional
   `basis` field dialect.
4. Why did no no-model canary catch this? Existing fixtures covered
   controlled terminal-state spelling and conflicts, not structured
   list-of-strings basis evidence.
5. What countermeasure prevents recurrence? Add a narrow Stage05 basis
   predicate/normalizer and neutral positive/negative fixtures: accept

5. What prevents recurrence? A shared Stage05 basis predicate now accepts only
   a non-empty string or non-empty list of non-empty strings, trims strings, and

The repair must not turn `basis` into a delta_result, terminal state, or
proof-by-token. It only preserves supporting evidence shape for an already
controlled terminal state.

### Adjacent Andon GATE64A-TST-STAGE03-M9-PREDICATION-MODE

Status:
Closed pushed/green by Gate64B. It was not patched in Gate64A to avoid
broadening the Stage05 basis repair, then was classified and repaired in the
separate owner-operation parity lane before another model matrix.

Symptom:
During Smoke A, direct handshake replay of the Gate64 TST failure record failed
before Stage05 basis validation because Stage03 carried executable
`owner_id: M9`, `operation: predication-mode`.

Failed check:

```powershell
python tools\check_staged_runtime_handshake.py --records .daee\release-regression-smokes\20260605-182026-matrix-trinitarian-j173\records\staged-handoff-failure.json .daee\release-regression-smokes\20260605-182026-matrix-tst-lillard\records\staged-handoff-failure.json
```

Failure text:
`stage-03 owner_routes[5]: operation token 'predication-mode' is outside
controlled operation vocabulary for M9; allowed: predication-repair, repair,
sense-split`.

Owner file / owner surface:
Stage03 owner-operation vocabulary and staged runner/checker parity for M9
predication operation tokens.

5 Whys:
1. Why did direct replay fail? The TST failure record used `predication-mode`
   as an executable Stage03 M9 operation token.
2. Why is that suspicious? `mode` is not the same DSL layer as callable owner
   operation; the controlled vocabulary expects `predication-repair`, `repair`,
   or `sense-split`.
3. Why was it not repaired here? The current blocking matrix pattern was the
   shared Stage05 basis evidence dialect, and broadening into M9 operation
   mapping risks mixing two root causes in one patch.
4. Why does it matter before another matrix? It may reveal another
   checker/producer asymmetry where the runner allowed a routed executable
   operation the handshake checker rejects.
5. What prevents recurrence? A separate no-model owner-operation parity lane
   should decide whether producer prompts must emit `predication-repair` or the
   controlled M9 vocabulary must formally admit a different operation token.

Hansei:
Do not paper over this with broad aliases. If `predication-mode` is merely a
module/mode label, it must not be accepted as a callable operation. If it is a
real operation, it needs an owner-local mapping, register-axis binding, and
delta vocabulary canaries.

Countermeasure:
Handled by the Gate64B owner-operation parity lane after Gate64A committed and
went green. The countermeasure keeps `predication-mode` out of executable M9
operation vocabulary and rewrites the producer prompt toward
`predication-repair`.

Smoke B command/result:
See Gate64B. Direct replay now fails for the intended preserved historical
reason while Stage03 owner-operation vocabulary parity passes the no-model
checks.

Remaining blocker or closure:
Closed by Gate64B (`6aa09fc` / `dc8041a`) before another model matrix.

## Gate64B Stage03 M9 Owner-Operation Parity Repair - Closed Pushed Green

Status:
Closed pushed/green for the adjacent TST Stage03 owner-operation parity
finding. Commit `6aa09fc` (`fix: enforce stage03 owner operation vocabulary`)
was pushed to `origin/main`; GitHub CI run `27045406409` and Pages run
`27045405927` both completed successfully on that head SHA.

Protected DSL transition:
`selected route -> owner_id -> callable owner operation -> Stage04 ACT row`.
Mode/module labels are not callable operations.

Symptom:
Direct handshake replay of the Gate64 TST failure record exposed a Stage03
route where `owner_id: M9` was marked executable with `operation:
predication-mode`. The checker rejected it, but the runner Stage03 normalizer
previously allowed the same row to pass Stage03.

Failed check:

```powershell

```

Failure text:
`stage-03 owner_routes[5]: operation token 'predication-mode' is outside
controlled operation vocabulary for M9; allowed: predication-repair, repair,
sense-split`.

Owner file / owner surface:
`tools/delta_result_vocabulary.py`,
`tools/run_staged_current_skill_smoke.py`,
`tools/check_staged_runtime_handshake.py`, and
`tests/staged-runtime-handshake/invalid/stage03-m9-mode-label-as-operation.json`.

Failure class:
producer/scaffold failed to enforce the DSL owner-operation vocabulary at
Stage03. The checker was already strict; the runner did not share that
predicate.

5 Whys:
1. Why did replay fail? The TST Stage03 route used `predication-mode` as a
   callable M9 operation.
2. Why is that invalid? `predication-mode` is a module/mode label; the M9
   owner-operation vocabulary licenses `predication-repair`, `repair`, and
   `sense-split`.
3. Why did the original smoke advance past Stage03? The runner normalized
   `owner_routes` without applying the same owner-operation vocabulary predicate
   the handshake checker applies.
4. Why was this not caught earlier? The Stage03 runner self-test covered rich
   route normalization and missing owner IDs, but not invalid executable
   owner-operation tokens.
5. What prevents recurrence? The owner-route vocabulary predicate now lives in
   `delta_result_vocabulary.py` and is used by both the runner and checker;
   runner self-tests and a neutral invalid handshake fixture reject
   `M9.predication-mode`.

Hansei:
Do not fix this with a broad alias or by admitting `predication-mode` as an
operation. If a future owner decision wants a new M9 operation, it needs a
source-owned vocabulary entry, register-axis mapping, delta vocabulary, and
fixtures. This repair preserves callable-operation formalism by rejecting the
mode label.

Countermeasure:
- Moved Stage03 executable owner-route vocabulary checks into the shared
  `delta_result_vocabulary.py` owner module.
- Updated `check_staged_runtime_handshake.py` to import the shared predicate.
- Updated `run_staged_current_skill_smoke.py` to reject invalid executable
  Stage03 owner operations during normalization.
- Added runner self-test canaries: valid `M9.predication-repair` passes;
  invalid `M9.predication-mode` fails.
- Added a neutral invalid staged-runtime fixture for mode-label-as-operation.

Anti-slimming check:
No model output was regenerated, shortened, or rewritten. The repair only
blocks an invalid Stage03 route operation token from becoming executable; it
does not reduce burden, generated-burden, submove, ACT, MRP/NAR, field_witness,
or public-output witness coverage.

Status:

prompt boundary`. This repair protects the one-shot matrix launch boundary. It
does not alter Stage01-08 proof semantics, owner/TTP routing, ACT rows,
delta_result validation, MRP/NAR, field_witness, public output, Stage08,
retained promotion, package, release, or provenance.

Symptom:
Gate64 Secularism exited before Stage01 because the subagent launch did not
preserve `/daee-epistemics refute secularism` as one `--raw-input` value.

`run_staged_current_skill_smoke.py: error: unrecognized arguments: refute secularism`.

Coordinator/process invocation failure. This was not a model semantic failure,
not a Stage01-08 checker failure, and not Secularism proof evidence.

5 Whys:

   command into more than one argv token.

   Secularism proof while preserving no diagnostic model artifact.
5. What countermeasure prevents recurrence? Use a path-backed prompt file for
   this slash-command smoke and run the generic `--preflight-input-only` command
   for every future matrix row before launching model subagents.

Hansei:
The runner already supported literal `--raw-input`, but the matrix coordinator
still relied on shell-sensitive quoting for the most compact raw slash-command
case. The next matrix must prefer path-backed prompt inputs for slash commands

(`fix: recognize proof-method carrier transitions`) was pushed to `main`; CI
run `27046929901` and Pages run `27046929714` both completed with
`success`. This closes the Gate64 Khaybar proof-method carrier/body-mass
checker gap against the preserved Gate64 output; it does not create a fresh
Khaybar model run, Stage08 sidecar, retained promotion, package, release, or
provenance claim.

Protected DSL transition:
`body_ref -> proof-method-audit owner operation -> proof-family-and-carrier-audit
-> delta_result=proof-family-carrier-typed -> dereferenced public body ->
MRP/manual-render/graph reconstructibility`. The repair keeps `body_ref` bare
and keeps the compact delta token as a projection of body-backed proof-method
carrier typing, not proof by token presence.

Symptom:
Gate64 Khaybar produced `output.md` and passed Stage01-06, but direct Stage07
validation rejected `ACT ⁴B₁[proof-method-audit.proof-family-and-carrier-audit]`
with `Δ=Δ⁴B:proof-family-carrier-typed`. The MRP checker reported that the
delta was not a concrete burden-local state change and that the dereferenced
body was not owner-specific. Manual render also rejected `⁴B` as
conclusion-shaped and insufficient for `Land(⁴B)`.

Failed checks:

```powershell
python tools\check_mrp_generated_burden.py --outputs .daee\release-regression-smokes\20260605-182026-matrix-khaybar\output.md --show-advisories
python tools\check_manual_smoke_render_contract.py --outputs .daee\release-regression-smokes\20260605-182026-matrix-khaybar\output.md
python tools\check_graph_completeness.py --outputs .daee\release-regression-smokes\20260605-182026-matrix-khaybar\output.md
```

Original failure text:
- `ACT ⁴B₁ Delta result must name a concrete burden-local state change`
- `ACT ⁴B₁ record alone does not pass; dereferenced body is not owner-specific`
- `Layer B submove mass insufficient: required fields are present, but owner/TTP work is conclusion-shaped rather than operation-shaped`
- `proof-method-audit submove did not audit the proof grammar or inferential method`
- `activation_verification=false`

Owner file / owner surface:
`tools/check_mrp_generated_burden.py`,
`tools/check_manual_smoke_render_contract.py`,
`tests/mrp-generated-burden/valid/proof-method-carrier-transition.md`,
`tests/mrp-generated-burden/invalid/proof-method-carrier-label-only.md`,
`tests/manual-smoke-render/valid/proof-method-carrier-transition.md`, and
`tests/manual-smoke-render/invalid/proof-method-carrier-label-only.md`.

Failure class:
Checker/canary under-recognition of a typed DSL transition. Gemba showed the
preserved Khaybar body did perform proof-method carrier work: it typed the logic
tree as a proof carrier, exposed dependence on premise strength/source sorting/
definition stability/entailment licensing, and blocked the formal display from
outranking its premises. The old generic `STATE_CHANGE_RE` did not recognize
this proof-method transition shape, and there were no positive/negative
proof-method carrier canaries to prevent checker/producer drift.

5 Whys:
1. Why did the preserved Khaybar output fail? The checker treated
   `proof-family-carrier-typed` as an unsupported compact delta and did not
   recognize the proof-carrier body transition.
2. Why was the body not recognized? The generic state-change predicate looked
   for broad words such as blocked/separated/identified, while proof-method
   carrier typing expressed the state change as no-longer-neutral proof,
   carrier dependency, and premise-set compression.
3. Why did manual render fail too? Manual render used the same generic
   operation-shaped mass logic and had no proof-method-specific transition
   predicate.
4. Why did no no-model canary catch this earlier? Existing proof-method
   vocabulary fixtures covered owner operation/delta availability, but not
   body-backed proof-family carrier typing versus label-only repetition.
5. What countermeasure prevents recurrence? Add a strict proof-method carrier
   predicate to both validators and neutral positive/negative fixtures proving
   that `proof-family-carrier-typed` passes only when the public body exposes
   carrier, dependency/premise, and state-change evidence.

Hansei:
The original Andon correctly refused token-only proof, but the checker lacked
the formal proof-method predicate needed to distinguish a real carrier audit
from a label-shaped row. The repair must not add generic prose tolerance or
accept `proof-family-carrier-typed` by token presence; it must require the
dereferenced body to perform the proof-method transition.

Countermeasure:
- Added a body-backed proof-method carrier transition predicate to
  `check_mrp_generated_burden.py`.
- Added the same public-render predicate to
  `check_manual_smoke_render_contract.py` so MRP and manual-render gates agree.
- Added neutral valid/invalid canaries for MRP generated-burden checking.
- Added neutral valid/invalid canaries for manual-render checking.
- Kept the invalid fixtures label-shaped but scaffold-complete so failure is
  caused by missing proof-method body evidence, not missing boilerplate.

Anti-slimming check:
No model output was regenerated, shortened, rewritten, accepted by omission, or
made thinner. The preserved Khaybar output is unchanged; the repair increases
checker specificity for a typed proof-method transition and keeps label-only
fixtures failing.

Smoke B command/result:

```powershell
python -m py_compile tools\check_mrp_generated_burden.py tools\check_manual_smoke_render_contract.py
python tools\check_mrp_generated_burden.py --outputs .daee\release-regression-smokes\20260605-182026-matrix-khaybar\output.md --show-advisories
python tools\check_manual_smoke_render_contract.py --outputs .daee\release-regression-smokes\20260605-182026-matrix-khaybar\output.md
python tools\check_graph_completeness.py --outputs .daee\release-regression-smokes\20260605-182026-matrix-khaybar\output.md
python tools\run_staged_current_skill_smoke.py --self-test
python tools\check_staged_runtime_handshake.py
python tools\check_compiled_runtime_freshness.py
```

Result:
PASS. The exact preserved Gate64 Khaybar output now passes MRP
generated-burden validation, manual-render validation, and graph completeness.
MRP fixtures report `22` valid / `48` invalid; manual-render fixtures report
`8` valid / `31` invalid; graph-completeness reports `8` valid / `34` invalid
and `1` hosted/live output checked.

Remaining blocker or closure:
Closed pushed/green for the Gate64 Khaybar proof-method carrier/body-mass
checker gap. This does not authorize Stage08, retained promotion, another
model matrix, package, tag, release, release assets, public sample publication,
or provenance.

Ledger/status-freshness drift. The proof/code fixes were already pushed green;
the canonical Markdown ledger had contradictory historical status prose.

   as open after later Gate64 rows closed them.

   not refresh upstream summaries and adjacent-finding text.

   blocker text can make the coordinator treat a closed blocker as still open.

   matrix ledger.

Closing a repair row is not enough when prior rows are still used as launch
guards. The ledger must remain readable as a current decision surface, not only
as an append-only chronology.

### Andon GATE65-TST-M3-CALLABLE-OWNER-VOCABULARY

Symptom:
The TST row reached Stage03 but did not produce `output.md`. The runner
rejected an executable owner route because `M3` was routed/loaded as executable
without controlled owner-operation vocabulary.

Failed check:

```text
stage-03 owner_routes[1]: executable owner route 'M3' has no controlled owner-operation vocabulary; route labels are not callable ACT owners
```

Owner file / owner surface:
`atomics/skill/references/diagnostics/delta-result-vocabulary.json`,
`tools/run_staged_current_skill_smoke.py`, `tools/check_staged_runtime_handshake.py`,
and the relevant M3 owner documentation.

Failure class:
Producer/scaffold failed to emit or expose the DSL owner-operation/delta
availability required before Stage04 execution.

5 Whys:
1. Why did TST stop? Stage03 marked `M3` executable, but no callable
   owner-operation vocabulary was registered for that owner.
2. Why was this invalid? A route label is not an ACT owner operation; Stage04
   needs `owner_id`, `operation`, approved `register_axis`, and owner-local
   `delta_result`.
3. Why did the system allow it to reach the gate? Delta vocabulary existed for
   the owner family, but executable owner-operation vocabulary was absent.
4. Why was it not caught before the live smoke? The canary set covered several
   owner/delta availability families, but did not include this `M3` executable
   owner selected-without-operation mapping shape.
5. What countermeasure prevents recurrence? Add neutral no-model canaries for
   executable owner routes with and without callable operation/delta mapping,
   and patch the owner mapping or HOLD/PARTIAL fallback at the owner source.

Hansei:
The system correctly refused route-label-as-owner execution, but the matrix
still spent a model row before the missing M3 executable operation mapping was
made visible. The next patch must close the checker-owned mapping/fallback
gap, not make Stage03 more permissive.

Countermeasure proposed:
Add or enforce a checker-owned mapping:
`owner_id / operation -> approved register_axis candidates -> approved
delta_result vocabulary -> executable Land allowed`. For `M3`, either register
strict callable owner operations with neutral canaries or keep the route
HOLD/PARTIAL until the owner body is executable.

Smoke B command/result:
Gate65 one-shot TST smoke produced this Andon. Repair Smoke B is pending in the
next no-model lane.

Remaining blocker or closure:
Open. This blocks another four-smoke matrix before source/checker/fixture
repair and pushed-green CI.

### Andon GATE65-SECULARISM-STAGE06-NFRAME-HELD-SHAPE

Symptom:
The Secularism row passed Stage01-05 but failed Stage06 because
`normalized_activation_record_details.n_frame_details.held` was emitted as an
object list with frame/reason detail instead of a string list.

Failed check:

```text
stage-06 normalized_activation_record_details.n_frame_details.held must be a string list when present
```

Owner file / owner surface:
`tools/run_staged_current_skill_smoke.py`, `tools/check_staged_runtime_handshake.py`,
and Stage06 runtime-handshake fixtures.

Failure class:
Producer/scaffold failed to preserve the typed Stage06 NAR schema boundary.

5 Whys:
1. Why did Secularism stop? Stage06 emitted held-frame details in the wrong
   shape.
2. Why was the shape wrong? The model supplied richer object detail in the
   `held` list rather than keeping `held` as frame-token strings.
3. Why is that unsafe? Consumers expect a stable NAR schema; embedding reasons
   in the token list breaks parity with the checker contract.
4. Why was this not caught before the model row? Existing Stage06 checks cover
   the string-list contract, but the matrix did not have a no-model producer
   canary for this object-list emission before live smoke.
5. What countermeasure prevents recurrence? Add a no-model Stage06 canary and,
   if needed, a narrow normalizer that separates held frame tokens from hold
   reasons without weakening `normalized_activation_record.n_frame`.

Hansei:
The stricter Stage06 schema exposed a real typed-boundary failure. The repair
must preserve detail without laundering schema drift through broader tolerance.

Countermeasure proposed:
Keep `n_frame_details.held` as a string list. Place hold reasons in a distinct
detail field or require the producer to emit the existing schema exactly. Add
neutral valid/invalid fixtures around this shape.

Smoke B command/result:
Gate65 one-shot Secularism smoke produced this Andon. Repair Smoke B is pending
in the next no-model lane.

Remaining blocker or closure:
Open. This blocks another four-smoke matrix before source/checker/fixture

### Andon GATE65-TRINITARIAN-M9-REPAIR-CONTRACT-SPLIT

Symptom:
The Trinitarian row produced `output.md` and passed Stage01-06, but Stage07
release validation failed because the output used `M9.repair` while the MRP
checker's source-owned operation registry accepts only `M9.predication-repair`
and `M9.sense-split`.

Failed check:

```powershell
python tools\check_mrp_generated_burden.py --outputs .daee\release-regression-smokes\20260605-203835-matrix-trinitarian-j173\output.md --show-advisories
```

Failure:

```text
ACT ⁵B₁ operation 'repair' is not a registered source-owned operation for 'M9'; expected one of: predication-repair, sense-split
```

Owner file / owner surface:
`tools/check_mrp_generated_burden.py`,
`atomics/skill/references/diagnostics/delta-result-vocabulary.json`, and
`tools/run_staged_current_skill_smoke.py` Stage04 owner-operation guidance.

Failure class:
Producer/checker contract split over a controlled owner operation. This is not
a permission to accept generic `repair` as proof.

5 Whys:
1. Why did Stage07 fail? The public ACT row used operation `repair` for `M9`,
   but the Stage07 checker registry does not consider that source-owned.
2. Why did the model emit `repair`? The Stage03/Stage04 guidance surface still
   exposes or tolerates `M9.repair` while the MRP checker expects more specific
   operations.
3. Why is this a release blocker? Generic `repair` can hide whether the owner
   performed predication, sense-splitting, residue reduction, or another typed
   register operation.
4. Why was it not caught earlier? The no-model canaries did not cover the
   producer/checker split for M9 operation vocabulary across guidance and
   Stage07 validation.
5. What countermeasure prevents recurrence? Align the M9 operation vocabulary
   across producer guidance and checkers with neutral canaries proving generic
   `repair` fails unless a body-backed formal mapping is explicitly approved.

Hansei:
The checker protected the formal DSL boundary, but producer guidance still let
generic operation vocabulary leak into public ACT. The repair must standardize
the owner vocabulary at the source, not relax Stage07.

Countermeasure proposed:
Prefer specific registered operations such as `predication-repair` or
`sense-split` for M9 work. If `repair` remains, it must become a controlled,
body-backed formal operation with register-axis and delta canaries; otherwise
remove it from executable guidance.

## Gate65A M3 Owner-Operation Availability Repair - Local Smoke B Passed

Status:
Local no-model repair complete with Smoke B passing; commit/push/CI/Pages
closure is pending at the time of this row. This lane addresses the earliest
Gate65 matrix blocker: TST stopped at Stage03 because `M3` was routed as
executable while the checker-owned vocabulary had M3 delta tokens but no
callable owner operation.

Attachment verification / current-truth classification:
- SOURCE formal-transition and `body_ref` layer-separation material:
  architectural invariant to preserve, not reopened by this lane.
- `V2.reconstituting-reason` and `pattern-profiling` Stage04 availability:
  already satisfied by current neutral staged-handshake canary
  `tests/staged-runtime-handshake/valid/stage04-owner-vocabulary-v2-pattern.json`.
- Older claim that TST currently fails Stage04 on V2 and Secularism currently
  fails Stage04 on pattern-profiling: stale/superseded by Gate65 Gemba.
- Current Gate65 TST blocker: still open and relevant before this patch.
- Anti-slimming/public projection material: architectural invariant to
  preserve, not directly implemented by this M3 mapping patch.

Protected DSL transition:
`burden_id -> selected M3 route -> callable M3.orphaned-intuition operation ->
bare body_ref ACT row -> owner-local delta_result -> Stage04 executable Land`.
The patch does not encode owner, operation, register axis, or `delta_result`
inside `body_ref`.

Symptom:
Gate65 TST reached Stage03 and failed before Stage04 because an executable
`M3` route had controlled owner-family delta vocabulary but no controlled
owner-operation vocabulary.

Failed check:

```text
stage-03 owner_routes[1]: executable owner route 'M3' has no controlled owner-operation vocabulary; route labels are not callable ACT owners
```

Owner file / owner surface:
`atomics/skill/references/diagnostics/delta-result-vocabulary.json`,
`tools/run_staged_current_skill_smoke.py`,
`tools/check_mrp_generated_burden.py`, and neutral staged-runtime-handshake
fixtures.

Failure class:
Producer/scaffold failed to emit or expose the DSL owner-operation/delta
availability required for Stage04 execution.

5 Whys:
1. Why did TST stop? Stage03 selected `M3` as executable, but `M3` lacked an
   `owner_operations` entry.
2. Why was that invalid? A route label is not a callable ACT operation; Stage04
   needs a controlled operation token paired with an owner-local delta.
3. Why did the system allow the ambiguity? M3 had `owner_families` delta tokens
   but no explicit operation vocabulary or register-axis mapping.
4. Why was it not caught earlier? Existing neutral canaries covered V2,
   pattern-profiling, generic unmapped owners, and HOLD/PARTIAL fallback, but
   not the M3 positive executable mapping.
5. What countermeasure prevents recurrence? Register `M3.orphaned-intuition`
   as the callable M3 operation, bind it to approved noetic register axes in
   the staged runner, enforce body-backed M3 operation recognition in the MRP
   checker, and add neutral positive/negative staged-handshake canaries.

Hansei:
The checker was right to reject route-label execution. The miss was that M3 was
partially registered: its deltas were controlled, but its callable operation
was not. The repair must finish the typed owner-operation mapping rather than
letting generic `repair` or route labels stand in for owner work.

## Gate65B Stage06 NAR Held-Frame Detail Shape Repair

Status:
In progress, no-model repair lane. This lane addresses the Gate65 Secularism
Stage06 `normalized_activation_record_details.n_frame_details.held` schema
Andon without rerunning a model smoke.

Attachment / Northstar classification:
The DSL/IR layer-separation invariant remains binding: join keys, owners,
operations, register axes, and delta results must stay separated. For this
Stage06 lane, the operative local schema claim is already satisfied and
preserved by current validators: canonical `n_frame` is a scalar string and
`n_frame_details.held` is a string list when present. The still-open relevant
gap is producer/canary guidance for preserving held-frame reasons without
putting reason objects inside the `held` string-list join surface. SOURCE
formal-transition work is preserved but not reopened here.

Symptom:
The Gate65 Secularism row reached Stage06 and emitted
`normalized_activation_record_details.n_frame_details.held` as an object list
with `n_frame` plus `hold_reason` entries.

Failed check:

```text
stage-06 normalized_activation_record_details.n_frame_details.held must be a string list when present
```

Owner file / owner surface:
`tools/run_staged_current_skill_smoke.py`,
`tools/check_staged_runtime_handshake.py`, and
`tests/staged-runtime-handshake/` Stage06 fixtures.

Failure class:
Producer/scaffold failed to preserve the Stage06 NAR DSL/schema split. Held
frame identity belongs in `n_frame_details.held` as string tokens; hold reasons
belong in a separate detail surface.

5 Whys:
1. Why did Secularism stop? Stage06 put held-frame reason objects into
   `n_frame_details.held`.
2. Why did that violate the contract? `held` is a string-list join/identity
   surface, not a detail-object list.
3. Why did the model choose the bad shape? The scaffold named selected/held
   details but did not explicitly name where hold reasons belong.
4. Why was this expensive to discover? The fixture suite lacked a neutral
   object-list `held` canary and a positive reason-preservation canary.
5. What prevents recurrence? Keep the checker strict, teach the scaffold to
   use `held_details[]`, and add valid/invalid no-model canaries.

Hansei:
Detail preservation is good, but placing the detail inside a join field breaks
the typed DSL boundary. The countermeasure must preserve richness through a
separate detail surface instead of accepting object-shaped `held`.

Countermeasure applied:
The Stage06 scaffold now states that `n_frame_details.held` must be a JSON
array of held n_frame string tokens only. Optional hold reasons go in
`n_frame_details.held_details[]` as objects with `n_frame` and `hold_reason`,
and each detail `n_frame` must also appear in `held`. The runner and staged
runtime checker validate that shape. Neutral fixtures cover a valid string-list
plus detail object shape, invalid object-list `held`, and invalid
`held_details` pointing outside the held-token list.

Local checker-crash Andon:
During the first targeted invalid-fixture probe, the standalone checker crashed
while aggregating the new malformed `held` fixture because it built
`held_tokens` after already detecting non-string list entries.

Failed command:

```powershell
python tools\check_staged_runtime_handshake.py --records tests\staged-runtime-handshake\valid\stage06-normalized-nframe-details.json tests\staged-runtime-handshake\invalid\stage06-nframe-details-held-object-list.json tests\staged-runtime-handshake\invalid\stage06-nframe-details-held-details-unlisted.json
```

Crash:

```text
AttributeError: 'dict' object has no attribute 'strip'
```

Checker-crash 5 Whys:
1. Why did the checker crash? It attempted `.strip()` on invalid object entries
   after appending a string-list error.
2. Why was that possible? Error aggregation continued as if `held` were a
   valid string list.
3. Why is that unsafe? Invalid fixtures should produce controlled checker
   errors, not infrastructure tracebacks.
4. Why was it not caught earlier? The invalid object-list canary did not exist.
5. What prevents recurrence? Build `held_tokens` only after the string-list
   predicate passes.

Checker-crash countermeasure:
Both runner and checker now derive `held_tokens` only from a validated
string-list `held`. The direct invalid-fixture probe now exits nonzero for the
expected invalid-record messages instead of crashing.

PASS:

```powershell
python tools\run_staged_current_skill_smoke.py --self-test
python tools\check_staged_runtime_handshake.py
python tools\check_mrp_generated_burden.py
python tools\check_manual_smoke_render_contract.py
python tools\check_compiled_runtime_freshness.py
python tools\build_docs_index.py --check
python tools\check_docs_index_interactions.py
python tools\check_docs_output_grapher_smoke.py
python tools\check_staged_runtime_handshake.py --records .daee\release-regression-smokes\20260605-203835-matrix-secularism\records\staged-handoff-failure.json
git diff --check
git diff --cached --check
```

5 Whys:

3. Why is that unsafe? `repair` does not identify whether M9 performed

   owner-shaped body.

Hansei:
The word "repair" is useful prose but too weak as an executable owner
operation. Keeping it in the operation slot invites conclusion-shaped ACT rows
that sound right while hiding which noetic register transition occurred.

1. Why did the sweep stop? Python received the literal glob `tools/*.py`.
2. Why did Python receive a literal glob? PowerShell did not expand that token
   when passed as an argument to `python -m py_compile`.
3. Why is this an Andon? Smoke B evidence must distinguish code failures from
   command invocation failures.
4. Why was it not caught earlier? The command was copied from the bash-style CI
   line without adapting it to the local PowerShell shell.
5. What prevents recurrence? Use PowerShell-expanded file arguments locally:
   `$files = Get-ChildItem tools -Filter *.py | ForEach-Object { $_.FullName };
   python -m py_compile @files`.

Countermeasure result:
The PowerShell-expanded `py_compile` command passed.

Remaining blocker or closure:
Locally closed pending safety scans, commit, push, and CI/Pages green. Fresh
Trinitarian model-smoke proof is still required before claiming the matrix
blocker is semantically closed.

### Gate66 Andon - Secularism Stage04 Canonical ACT Row Dialect

Symptom:
Secularism failed before Stage05/06 because Stage04 emitted raw ACT rows but the
first row was not parseable as canonical ACT.

Failed check:
`stage-04 act_rows[0] is not a parseable canonical ACT row`.

Owner file / owner surface:
`tools/run_staged_current_skill_smoke.py` Stage04 producer prompt/scaffold and
canonical ACT row parser/normalizer surfaces; no source/checker patch applied in
this matrix lane.

5 Whys:
1. Why did Secularism fail? The Stage04 model response emitted at least one ACT
   row outside the canonical parser grammar.
2. Why did that block execution? Stage04 cannot accept ACT execution unless the
   row exposes owner, operation/register pressure, bare `body_ref`, delta, and
   Land/HOLD status in parser-stable form.
3. Why did the model produce a dialect row? The scaffold still allows enough
   prose-shaped freedom that the model can write ACT-looking text without the
   strict row shape.
4. Why was this not caught before the model smoke? Existing canaries cover many
   owner-operation and body_ref risks, but not this neutral raw Secularism-like
   canonical ACT syntax miss as a Stage04 producer failure.
5. What prevents recurrence? Add a no-model Stage04 fixture/canary that rejects
   ACT-like prose rows and accepts only the canonical `⟦ACT ... :: π=... ::
   body_ref=... :: Δ=... :: Land(Bn)⟧` shape with strict owner-operation mapping.

Hansei:
The M9 repair strengthened owner-operation vocabulary but did not guarantee that
all future Stage04 rows stay parseable before semantic owner checks begin.

Countermeasure:
Open a narrow no-model Secularism/neutral Stage04 ACT syntax repair lane before
any further model matrix. Do not loosen parser acceptance; repair the producer
scaffold/canary boundary.
